## Q23: What is the best farming setup for early-game players (Farming 1-20)? What tools, pets, and strategies should they use?

**Answer:**

For players in the early game (Farming levels 1–20), the primary focus should be on transitioning from the Hub to the **Garden** and establishing a foundation for skill progression rather than raw coin generation [1-3].

### **Essential Tools**
*   **Rookie Hoe:** This is the best starting tool, which can be purchased from the Farm Merchant in the Hub and should be enchanted with **Harvesting** [4].
*   **Basic and Advanced Gardening Hoes:** Available at the **Sky Mart** for a total of 25 copper, these are excellent "all-rounder" tools that provide farming fortune until you can afford specialized equipment [5].
*   **Specialized Tools:** As soon as possible, you should aim to obtain crop-specific tools (like **Euclid’s Wheat Hoe** or the **Fungi Cutter**) from the Sky Mart for 250 copper each, as they offer significantly higher fortune for their respective crops [5, 6].
*   **Enchantments:** Ensure your tools have the **Harvesting** enchantment; note that the **Sunder** enchantment has been removed from the game as it was redundant with Harvesting [5, 7].

### **Best Pets**
*   **Rabbit Pet:** This is the **most important pet** for early game because it provides **Farming Wisdom** (up to 30% at level 100), which speeds up your level progression more effectively than fortune-based pets [3, 8].
*   **Bee Pet:** A rare Bee pet from the Hub NPC is a great secondary choice, offering early combat stats and **fortune** to increase bonus drops while farming [9].
*   **Grandma Wolf:** This is a valuable **free pet** that can be obtained early in the Spider's Den to help with initial progression [10, 11].

### **Armor Progression**
*   **Farm Suit / Farm Armor:** These are your first sets, unlocked through the **Wheat Collection**; they provide basic survivability and SkyBlock XP but should be replaced quickly [2, 12].
*   **Melon Armor:** This is the **major goal** for the early game, unlocked at **Garden Level 6** [13]. It requires 120 enchanted melon blocks to craft and is the base set that eventually upgrades into the best farming gear in the game [13, 14].
*   **Rancher's Boots:** These are highly recommended because they allow you to **cap your speed**, which is necessary for maintaining the correct angles for efficient farming [15, 16].

### **Key Strategies**
*   **The Hub Start:** Begin by farming wheat in the Hub fields to reach **Wheat Collection III** and craft your first Farm Suit [12, 17, 18].
*   **Unlock the Garden:** You unlock the Garden at **SkyBlock Level 5** by talking to Sam; follow her introductory quests to learn the basic layout [2, 19].
*   **Garden Milestones & Visitors:** Level up your Garden by reaching **crop milestones** (starting at 100 crops broken) and completing **visitor offers**, which grant Garden XP and Copper [2, 20, 21].
*   **Jacob’s Farming Contests:** Participate in these competitions every 40 minutes to earn **medals** and **Jacob's Tickets**, which are required to buy specialized tools and "Turbo" enchantment books [22-24].
*   **Build Efficient Farms:** Use Garden plots to build "max efficiency" infinite farms; adding a **glass roof** over these farms can help make later pest farming much easier [16, 25, 26].
*   **Utility Items:** Use **Agronomy Sacks** to manage your inventory and use the **Composter** with organic matter and fuel (like Sunflower Oil) to unlock more plots [23, 27, 28].

---

## Q24: What is the best farming setup for mid-game players (Farming 20-40)? What armor, pets, tools, and enchants are optimal?

**Answer:**

For mid-game players with Farming levels 20 to 40, the optimal setup focuses on balancing crop yield (Farming Fortune) with leveling speed (Farming Wisdom) while transitioning through specialized armor sets [1, 2].

### **Armor Progression**
Mid-game farming is primarily defined by the progression from **Melon to Fermento armor** [1]. Each set requires specific rare drops obtained while farming certain crops in the previous tier:
*   **Level 25: Melon Armor.** Unlocked in the Melon collection. It is the base for all subsequent mid-game sets [3].
*   **Level 30: Croppie Armor.** Crafted using Melon armor and **Croppies**, which drop from wheat, carrots, or potatoes while wearing Melon armor [4, 5].
*   **Level 35: Squash Armor.** Crafted from Croppie armor and **Squash**, which drop from pumpkin, melon, and cocoa beans [5].
*   **Level 40: Fermento Armor.** Crafted from Squash armor and **Fermento**, which drop from sugarcane, cactus, mushrooms, and nether wart [5, 6].

**Essential Footwear:**
*   **Rancher's Boots:** Highly recommended over the full set boots to allow precise speed control, which is necessary for different crop layouts [4, 7].
*   **Sundial:** If you have a Sundial (requires Garden level 12 and chloronite), you can set your speed through the garden desk instead, allowing you to wear a full 4/4 set of armor for higher rare drop chances [2, 6, 8].

### **Optimal Pets**
*   **Rabbit Pet (Legendary):** The best choice for leveling. At level 100, it grants **30 Farming Wisdom** [2, 9].
*   **Elephant Pet (Legendary):** The standard for maximum Farming Fortune [2]. It should be equipped with a **Green Bandana** (rare visitor drop) or a **Yellow Bandana** for additional fortune [5, 10].
*   **Mushroom Cow Pet (Legendary):** Often outperforms the Elephant if the player has a high **Strength** stat, as it grants fortune based on strength and yields extra mushrooms [11, 12].
*   **Hedgehog Pet:** Used specifically for **Pest Farming** to gain extra fortune and efficiency when killing pests like earthworms [11, 13].

### **Tools and Specialized Equipment**
All farming hoes and axes have been recategorized into a single **"Farming Tool"** type [14].
*   **Designated Crop Tools:** You should aim for the specific tool for the crop you are farming (e.g., **Euclid’s Wheat Hoe**, **Fungi Cutter**). These are purchased for 250 copper at the Skymart and have three upgrade tiers [15-17].
*   **Lotus/Blossom Equipment:** Progress through the **Lotus set** (unlocked at 50 to 500 visitor offers), which gives fortune based on your total visitor milestones [18]. The **Blossom set** is a direct upgrade [19].

### **Optimal Enchantments and Reforges**
**Tool Enchantments:**
*   **Harvesting VI:** Standard for all farming tools [20].
*   **Cultivation:** Essential for long-term progression as it grants more fortune the more you farm with the tool [21, 22].
*   **Dedication IV:** Provides high fortune based on your crop milestones [20, 23].
*   **Turbo-Crop V:** Specific to the crop you are farming, obtained from Jacob's contests [24].
*   **Crop Fever (Ultimate Enchant):** Grants a chance to trigger a 60-second "fever" providing **100 Farming Fortune** and a 15% bonus for rare items like Croppies or Squash [7, 25].

**Reforges:**
*   **Blessed:** The most reliable for mid-game, providing both fortune and a chance to drop enchanted crops [26].
*   **Bustling (Armor):** A common mid-game choice applied with Skymart Brochures [27].
*   **Rooted (Equipment):** The best reforge for equipment, applied with a **Burrowing Spore** [23].
*   **Overpriced (New Meta):** A recently introduced reforge that prioritizes rare crop drops [26, 28].

### **Summary Table for Mid-Game (Farming 20-40)**
| Category | Optimal Item(s) | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Armor** | Melon $\rightarrow$ Croppie $\rightarrow$ Squash $\rightarrow$ Fermento | Switch tiers every 5 levels [5]. |
| **Boots** | Rancher's Boots | Crucial for speed tuning [4]. |
| **Pet** | Rabbit (Wisdom) or Elephant/Cow (Fortune) | Use Rabbit until level 50/60 [2, 9]. |
| **Tool Reforge** | Blessed | Best balance of fortune and wisdom [23, 26]. |
| **Equipment** | Lotus or Blossom Set | Scales with total visitor offers [18, 19]. |
| **Enchants** | Harvesting, Dedication, Crop Fever | Focus on maxing these for your main tool [23, 25]. |

## Q25: What is the best endgame farming setup (Farming 50+)? What is Helianthus armor, when should players switch to it, and what does it cost?

**Answer:**

The best endgame farming setup (Farming 50+) centers on **Helianthus armor**, which is currently the highest-tier agricultural gear in the game [1, 2]. To optimize for maximum profit, players typically engage in **pest farming**, which can generate between 60 million and 90 million coins per hour depending on active Mayor perks [3, 4].

### **Helianthus Armor and Switching Requirements**
**Helianthus armor** is the progression upgrade following Fermento armor [1, 5]. It is specifically designed to drop its namesake material, **Helianthus**, while farming the newer crops: Wild Roses, Sunflowers, and Moonflowers [1, 6]. Players should switch to Helianthus armor once they reach **Farming Level 50**, as this is the level requirement for the upgrade from Fermento [1].

### **Cost Analysis**
The cost of the armor varies significantly based on how "hypermaxed" the pieces are:
*   **Hypermaxed Set:** A full set of **Mossy Helianthus armor** with Recombobulators and **Perfect Paradox gemstones** costs approximately **700 million coins** [2, 7].
*   **Budget Alternative:** Players can save about 120 million coins by using three-quarters Helianthus armor and replacing the boots with **Rancher's Boots** [8]. This sacrifice results in losing approximately 15 pest chance and 20 farming fortune but allows for easier speed control without a Sundial [8].

### **Best Endgame Farming Setup**
A complete endgame setup for players at Farming 50+ includes the following components:

*   **Armor:** A full set of **Mossy Helianthus armor** [2]. If using the full set, a **Sundial** (costing ~12 million coins) is mandatory to set the player's walking speed to the specific meta-speed for each crop [2, 8].
*   **Farming Tools:** Players should use specialized **Euclid's hoes** (or equivalent crop-specific tools) leveled to **Level 50** [8, 9]. Essential enchants include **Crop Fever 5**, **Dedication 4**, **Turbo-crop 5**, and **Harvesting 6** [8, 10]. For maximum fortune, tools should be slotted with **four Perfect Paradox gemstones** [7].
*   **Equipment:** The **Blossom equipment set** provides the highest farming fortune, though the **Lotus set** is a viable alternative [11]. For pest-specific farming, the **Pest Vest** and **David’s Cloak** are recommended for extra hunting fortune [1, 11]. All pieces should have the **Rooted reforge** and the **Green Thumb** enchantment [11, 12].
*   **Pets:**
    *   **Rose Dragon:** The definitive best-in-slot pet, though it costs over **2 billion coins** [13].
    *   **Mushroom Cow:** The second-best option for pure farming fortune, provided the player has maximized their **Strength** stat [14].
    *   **Hedgehog:** Used specifically when killing pests to provide an additional 100 farming fortune [13, 15].
    *   **Mosquito:** Preferred for spawning pests as it increases **Bonus Pest Chance** and makes vinyls 50% more effective [11].
*   **Garden Upgrades:** Players must maximize their **Garden Chips**, prioritizing the **Vermin Vaporizer** (for pest chance) and **Hypercharge** (to buff temporary fortune from NPCs like Phillip) [15, 16]. **Paradox attributes** such as the **Pest Shard** and **Crop Petal Shard** should also be maxed to increase rare loot drops [17].

## Q26: Compare the Elephant pet vs Mooshroom Cow pet for farming. Which gives more Farming Fortune, which gives more coins, and which should players use at each stage?

**Answer:**

The comparison between the **Elephant pet** and the **Mooshroom Cow pet** in 2026 centers on whether a player prioritizes raw collection materials or maximum profit through **Farming Fortune** scaling and extra drops [1, 2].

### **Farming Fortune Comparison**
*   **Mooshroom Cow:** This is considered the **second best pet in the game for actual Farming Fortune** when farming crops, surpassed only by the extremely expensive Rose Dragon pet [2]. Its strength lies in its ability to convert **Strength into Farming Fortune** (at a rate of 1 FF per 20 Strength) [2]. To maximize this, players must use Strength-focused setups, such as tuning points into Strength and using the **Acacia Birdhouse power stone** [2].
*   **Elephant:** While the Elephant was historically the king of Farming Fortune, it has lost some of its general dominance to other pets [3]. However, it remains a **top-tier choice for Ironman players** who need to hit high fortune milestones to gather materials for armor upgrades (like Melon to Crappie armor) before they have the stats to fully utilize a Mooshroom Cow's Strength scaling [1, 4].

### **Coin Generation**
*   **Mooshroom Cow is the superior pet for raw coin generation.** In addition to its high Farming Fortune, it grants a **bonus mushroom drop** every time a crop is broken [2]. These mushrooms significantly increase the total value of every farming session [2].
*   **Elephant** has transitioned into a more specialized role. With the addition of "Trunk Polish," it has found a niche in gathering **Sowdust**, a currency introduced in the Greenhouse update used for upgrading Garden Chips [3, 5]. While it may not produce as many raw coins as the Mooshroom Cow, it is valuable for players focusing on **Garden progression and Chip maxing** [3].

### **Which to Use at Each Stage**
The current meta for farming progression suggests the following pet priority:

*   **Early Game:** Players should focus on the **Rabbit pet** [1, 6]. At this stage, the most important goal is gaining **Farming Wisdom** to level up the Farming skill and unlock better crops and Garden plots, rather than maximizing raw crop yield [1, 6].
*   **Mid-Game:** 
    *   **Standard Profiles:** Transition to the **Mooshroom Cow** as soon as you have enough **Magical Power** and tuning points to make the Strength-to-Fortune conversion effective [2].
    *   **Ironman Profiles:** Stick with the **Legendary Elephant** [1, 4]. Ironman players typically hit Farming Level milestones before they can gather the materials for high-tier armor, making the Elephant's consistent base fortune more reliable for grinding materials [4].
*   **Late/Endgame:** 
    *   **Crop Farming:** The **Rose Dragon** (costing over 2 billion coins) is the absolute best-in-slot [2, 7]. Until then, a maxed **Mooshroom Cow** remains the standard [2].
    *   **Pest Farming Meta:** In 2026, many players use **Auto-Pet rules** to swap between their main fortune pet (Cow or Elephant) and specialized pets [5, 8]. You should use a **Mosquito or Slug pet** to increase bonus pest spawn chance, then rod-swap to a **Hedgehog pet** to kill the pests for maximum drops, before returning to your Mooshroom Cow for crop farming [5, 7-11].

## Q27: What farming tools are best at each stage? Compare: rookie hoe, advanced hoe, Euclid's Shovel, Theoretical Hoe, and specialized hoes per crop.

**Answer:**

In the 2026 Hypixel SkyBlock meta, farming tools have been unified into a single "Farming Tool" category, meaning hoes and axes now share the same enchantments (like Harvesting) and reforges [1, 2]. Progression generally moves from general-purpose starter tools to highly specialized mathematical and crop-specific gear.

### **Early Game: Generalist Tools**
At this stage, your focus is on unlocking the Garden and starting basic collections.

*   **Rookie Hoe:** This is the entry-level tool purchased from the **Farm Merchant** in the Hub [3]. It is best for players just starting their first wheat collection [4].
*   **Advanced Gardening Hoe:** Unlocked at the **Sky Mart** for a small amount of Copper (25 total if upgrading from the Basic version), this is a significant step up [4].
    *   **Comparison:** Unlike the Rookie Hoe, the Advanced Gardening Hoe provides Farming Fortune for all crops [4]. It is considered a "meta" choice for the majority of the early game because it allows you to progress multiple collections without needing a separate tool for every crop [4, 5].

### **Mid-Game: Specialized "Theoretical" Hoes**
As you aim for higher tiers in Jacob's Farming Contests, you must transition to crop-specific specialized tools [4].

*   **Theoretical (Mathematical) Hoes:** These are crop-specific hoes that have three tiers (T1, T2, and T3) [2, 6].
    *   **Euclid’s Wheat Hoe:** Used specifically for wheat [2, 7].
    *   **Other variants:** Gauss (Carrot), Pythagorean (Potato), Newton (Nether Wart), and Turing (Sugar Cane) [2].
*   **Other Specialized Tools:** For crops not covered by the Mathematical line, you should use dedicated items like the **Fungi Cutter** (Mushrooms), **Cactus Knife**, **Cocoa Chopper**, or **Pumpkin/Melon Dicers** (v1.0 to v3.0) [2].
    *   **Comparison:** These specialized tools provide significantly more Farming Fortune for their specific crop than the Advanced Gardening Hoe [5]. They also level up from **0 to 40** (capping at **50** with overclockers), gaining more stats as you use them [6].

### **End Game: Hyper-Maxed Tools**
At the endgame level, you utilize T3 versions of specialized tools with maximum enchantments and the latest 2026 upgrades.

*   **T3 Mathematical Hoes & 3.0 Dicers:** Items like the **Euclid’s Wheat Hoe MK3** are the gold standard for max yield [2, 7].
*   **Eclipse & Wild Rose Hoes:** These are the specialized tools for the newest crops added in the Greenhouse and Harvest Feast updates (Sunflowers, Moonflowers, and Wild Roses) [2, 8].
*   **Maximum Optimization:**
    *   **Ultimate Enchantments:** **Crop Fever** is the premier end-game enchant, granting a chance to trigger a "fever" for +100 Farming Fortune and +15% rare item drop chance [9].
    *   **Reforges:** While **Blessed** and **Bountiful** remain staples, new reforges like **Deep Fried** (applied with Deepfries) and **Overpriced** (applied with Overpriced Drink) offer specific buffs for seasoning and rare crop drop chances [10, 11].
    *   **Gems:** Hyper-maxed tools use **Perfect Paradox Gemstones** for maximum Farming Fortune [12].

### **Summary Comparison Table**

| Tool Stage | Tool Name | Primary Use | Strategic Advantage |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Beginner** | **Rookie Hoe** | Wheat / Early Collections | Extremely cheap starter [3] |
| **Early** | **Advanced Gardening Hoe** | All Crops | Generalist; good fortune for the price [4] |
| **Mid-Game** | **T1-T2 Math Hoes / Dicers** | Specific Crops | Higher specific fortune; starts tool leveling [6] |
| **End-Game** | **T3 Math Hoes / Eclipse Hoe** | Max Yield / New Crops | Highest fortune; supports Ultimate Enchants [2, 7] |

## Q28: What are the best farming enchantments? List the priority order for: Cultivating, Dedication, Harvesting, Replenish, Turbo-Crop variants, and others.

**Answer:**

Based on the sources, the best farming enchantments are prioritized by their impact on efficiency, quality of life, and **Farming Fortune**. Following the recent technical modernization of the game, several enchantments have been consolidated or expanded.

### **Farming Enchantment Priority Order**

1.  **Replenish**
    *   **Priority:** Essential / Highest.
    *   **Function:** Automatically replants the crop you just harvested [1]. Without this, farming is incredibly inefficient as you would have to manually replant your fields [1].

2.  **Harvesting**
    *   **Priority:** High / Core Fortune.
    *   **Function:** The standard enchantment for hoes and now **axes**, following the removal of the **Sunder** enchantment [2, 3]. It provides a direct increase to Farming Fortune [4].
    *   **Note:** While level V is standard, **Harvesting VI** is a rare reward, notably from events like the **Year of the Pig** [5-7].

3.  **Turbo-Crop Variants (e.g., Turbo-Wheat, Turbo-Cane)**
    *   **Priority:** High / Accessible Fortune.
    *   **Function:** Grants a specific amount of fortune for a particular crop [8]. Tiers I–V are obtained through **Jacob’s Farming Contests**, providing up to +25 fortune [8, 9].
    *   **Update:** The **Harvest Feast** event introduced **Turbo-Crop VI and VII**, further increasing the maximum possible fortune per crop [10, 11].

4.  **Dedication**
    *   **Priority:** Late-game / High Ceiling.
    *   **Function:** Provides fortune that scales with your **Garden Crop Milestones** [12, 13].
    *   **Note:** While levels I–III are accessible, **Dedication IV** is a high-priority endgame goal; it is a rare drop from the **Rhino** visitor [14, 15].

5.  **Cultivating**
    *   **Priority:** Long-term Scaling.
    *   **Function:** Increases Farming Fortune based on the total number of crops farmed with that specific tool [12].
    *   **Update:** It is recommended to apply this early because it requires time to level up, though the admins recently **lowered the crop requirements** needed to reach higher tiers [16, 17].

---

### **Other Important Enchantments & Enhancements**

*   **Crop Fever (Ultimate Enchantment):** This is the definitive ultimate enchantment for farming tools [18]. It has a small chance to trigger a 60-second "fever," granting **+100 Farming Fortune** and a **15% increased chance for rare drops** like Croppies, Squash, and Fermentos [12, 18].
*   **Feast:** A new enchantment added with the **Harvest Feast** event [19]. Applied to farming tools, it grants a **+0.5-2.5% rare crop chance**, which is vital for dropping event-specific materials like **Seasoning** and **Kernels** [19].
*   **Green Thumb:** An enchantment for **equipment slots** (Necklace, Cloak, etc.) [20]. It increases Farming Fortune based on your total completed **visitor offers** [20].
*   **Bug Blender:** A specialized enchantment for **vacuums** that grants up to **+20 Farming Fortune** specifically when vacuuming pests [21].
*   **Delicate:** Not explicitly listed in your list but critical for certain crops; it prevents you from accidentally breaking the stem of crops like pumpkins or melons [22].

### **Recommended Tool Reforges**
While not enchantments, these are prioritized alongside them to maximize yield:
*   **Blessed:** The standard choice for most players, providing both Farming Fortune and **Farming Wisdom** [15, 23].
*   **Bountiful:** Generally considered the "meta" for raw profit as it grants coins directly when harvesting crops, though it is more difficult to unlock [15, 23].
*   **Deep Fried:** A new reforge from the Harvest Feast that increases the odds of finding event seasonings by **25%** [10].

## Q29: What are the best farming reforges for maximizing Farming Fortune?

**Answer:**

For maximizing **Farming Fortune (FF)** in the early 2026 meta, the best reforges are often divided between general farming and specialized events like the **Harvest Feast**.

### **Farming Tool Reforges**
With the introduction of the Harvest Feast event, new reforges have challenged the long-standing dominance of the Blessed and Bountiful reforges.

*   **Overpriced:** This is widely considered the **new high-end meta** for tools [1]. Applied via the **Overpriced Drink**, it grants **+25 Farming Fortune** and increases the odds of finding rare crops by 7% [2].
*   **Hashbrown:** Applied using the **Hashbrown** item, this reforge provides **+20 Farming Fortune** and increases the chance of dropping seasoning (used for the Communal Stew) by 25% [2]. It is highly effective for players focusing on event progression [3].
*   **Bountiful:** Traditionally the best for pure profit, it is applied using a **Golden Ball** [4]. Recent updates moved its Farming Fortune from a reforge bonus to a base stat for aesthetic clarity [5].
*   **Blessed:** Applied using a **Blessed Fruit**, this remains a solid choice for those who still need **Farming Wisdom** [4]. The reforge bonus was recently changed to grant a chance for dropping enchanted crops while farming [5].

### **Armor Reforges**
*   **Mossy:** This remains the **absolute best reforge** for Farming Fortune on armor [6, 7]. It is applied using **Overgrown Grass**, which is a rare drop from visitors [6].
*   **Bustling:** A much more accessible budget option, applied using a **Sky Mart Brochure**. While it gives less fortune than Mossy, it is significantly cheaper [6].
*   **Manded:** If you are specifically **Pest Farming**, the Manded reforge (applied via the **Manded Claw**) is the top choice for your secondary pest-hunting armor set [8].

### **Equipment Reforges**
For your necklace, belt, cloak, and bracelet, the following are the primary options:

*   **Rooted:** This is the **best reforge for maximizing Farming Fortune** on equipment [4, 9]. It is applied using a **Burrowing Spore**, which is a rare drop from farming mushrooms [4].
*   **Blooming:** A cheaper alternative applied via a **Flowering Bouquet**, which is occasionally offered by visitors [4].
*   **Squeaky:** This reforge is specifically used on **Pest Hunter equipment** to maximize rates while hunting [10].

### **Vacuum Reforges (Pest Farming)**
*   **BD:** For your vacuum (such as the Infinity Vacuum Hooverus), **BD** is the definitive best reforge, as it grants a massive **+100 Farming Fortune on pests** [11, 12]. It is crafted using feast pumpkin drops, sugar, and a bottle [1].

***

*Note: Several of these reforges, specifically Overpriced and Hashbrown, were introduced as part of the Harvest Feast event on the Alpha Network in late March 2026 and may be subject to further balancing [13, 14].*

## Q30: Explain the Farmhand skill bonus. How much FF does each Farming level give and what is the max at Farming 60?

**Answer:**

The **Farmhand** skill bonus (the base attribute granted by leveling the Farming skill) provides a permanent increase to your **Farming Fortune (FF)** as you progress. 

Based on the sources, each **Farming level** grants **4 Farming Fortune** [1, 2]. This means that at the current level cap of **Farming 60**, a player receives a total bonus of **240 Farming Fortune** [1, 2]. 

This bonus is a significant part of a player's total farming stats, which can exceed 2,100 FF in hyper-optimized setups [3, 4]. Other items can scale with this level as well; for instance, **Farmer's Boots** provide an additional **1 Farming Fortune per Farming level** [5].

## Q31: Explain Jacob's Farming Contests completely. How do medals work (Bronze/Silver/Gold/Diamond), how is score calculated, and what are the best strategies for getting Diamond medals?

**Answer:**

**Jacob’s Farming Contests** are recurring, high-stakes competitions in which players compete to harvest the most of three randomly selected crops within a **20-minute window** [1-3]. These contests occur every **40 minutes** and are accessible to any player who has unlocked the Garden [1, 2].

### **Medal Tiers and Brackets**
Medals are awarded based on a player's final placement relative to all participants in that specific crop's contest. The current brackets for standard medals are:
*   **Bronze:** Top 60% of participants [2].
*   **Silver:** Top 30% of participants [2].
*   **Gold:** Top 10% of participants [2].
*   **Diamond:** This is the highest tier, typically requiring a placement among the elite top players (e.g., placing 11th out of thousands of participants) [4].

Earning these medals rewards you with **Jacob’s Tickets** and the medals themselves, which are used to purchase and upgrade specialized farming tools at the Sky Mart [2, 5, 6].

### **How Score is Calculated**
The score is determined by the total number of the designated **crops broken** and added to your inventory during the contest [2, 7]. Your score is not just the number of blocks broken, but the total yield, which is heavily amplified by your **Farming Fortune** [8, 9].

*   **Farming Fortune:** This stat provides a chance to obtain multiple crops from a single block break [8, 9]. For example, having high fortune can result in millions of items (like melon slices) being counted toward your score even if you only farm for a portion of the contest [4].
*   **Event-Specific Buffs:** The **Overdrive Chip** (unlocked via the Greenhouse update) can provide up to **+140 Farming Fortune** specifically for crops that are active in a current Jacob's contest [10].

### **Best Strategies for Getting Diamond Medals**
To reach the Diamond tier, you must move beyond standard farming and leverage endgame mechanics:

*   **Greenhouse Mutation "Bombing":** The most powerful strategy is to time the harvest of **Greenhouse mutations** with the start of a contest [11]. Mutations like **Chocoberries** or **Dusk Blooms** drop a massive base amount of crops when broken (e.g., one player broke enough mutations to gain **3.8 million melon slices** instantly) [4, 12, 13]. This provides an insurmountable head start over players who are farming manually from zero [4, 11].
*   **Maximized Gear and Reforges:** Wear a full set of **Helanthis armor** (the current best-in-slot set) or **Fermento armor** [14, 15]. Ensure your gear is reforged with **Mossy** (from Overgrown Grass) for maximum fortune [9].
*   **Specialized Farming Tools:** Use a **Tier 3 crop-specific tool** (like the Newton Nether Warts Hoe) that is fully enchanted with **Harvesting VI**, **Dedication IV**, and the relevant **Turbo-crop** book [6, 16, 17]. 
*   **Stat Optimization:**
    *   **Sundial:** Use a Sundial to set your exact walking speed to the "meta" speed for each crop to ensure you break exactly 20 blocks per second without missing any [15, 16].
    *   **Squeaky Mouse Mat:** Use this to snap your character to the perfect yaw and pitch angles required for max-efficiency farm designs [18, 19].
*   **Temporary Fortune Boosts:**
    *   **Harvest Harbinger V Potion:** Provides +50-100 Farming Fortune for 25 minutes [9, 20].
    *   **Pest Hunter Phillip:** Exchange 40 captured pests to "empty your vacuum bag," which grants a temporary buff of **+200 to +400 Farming Fortune** [9, 20, 21].

## Q32: What are all the Jacob Contest perks and in what order should players unlock them? What does each perk do?

**Answer:**

Based on the sources, perks related to the Jacob Farming Contests are primarily managed through **Anita** (located in the Hub's Farm Merchant's Dwelling) using medals and tickets, as well as the newer **Grand Bakery** (Kernel-based rewards) and **Carnival Perks**.

### **Jacob Contest Perks (Anita Upgrades)**
Anita offers permanent account-wide upgrades in exchange for **Gold, Silver, and Bronze medals**, alongside **Jacob's Tickets** [1, 2].

*   **Farming Level Cap:** Increases the maximum possible Farming skill level beyond 50, up to a current cap of **Farming 60** [3, 4].
*   **Extra Farming Fortune:** Provides permanent increases to your Farming Fortune stat. Each level typically grants **+4 Farming Fortune** across all crops, helping players reach higher brackets in contests [5, 6].
*   **Medal Exchange:** Anita allows players to upgrade or trade medals (e.g., converting multiple Bronze medals into Silver, or Silver into Gold) to afford higher-tier rewards [7].
*   **Specialized Farming Tools:** While technically items, Anita sells "Mathematical Blueprints" and specialized tools (like Euclid’s Wheat Hoe or Gauss Carrot Hoe) that can be upgraded through three tiers using tickets and specific crop collections [6, 8].

### **The Grand Bakery Rewards (Kernel Perks)**
Introduced in the 2026 Harvest Feast updates, these items are "baked" using **Kernels** earned during the event [9].

*   **Freshly Baked Accessories:** A talisman line (up to Heirloom rarity) that grants **+1–5% Rare Crop chance** [10].
*   **Feast I Books:** Enchantments for farming tools that grant **+0.5–2.5% Rare Crop chance** [10].
*   **Ted’s Abiphone Contact:** Unlocks an Abiphone contact who calls to identify which of the three in-season crops is granting **10% more Seasonings** [10].
*   **Scott’s Abiphone Contact:** Unlocks an Abiphone contact that allows the player to **open the Grand Bakery** even when the Grand Feast event is not active [10].
*   **Finn’s Focaccia:** A consumable granting a **+5% Rare Crop chance** for the remainder of the Grand Feast [10].

### **Carnival Perks (Doug’s Shop)**
These perks specifically boost efficiency during the **Harvest Feast** yearly event [11].

*   **Natural Talent I-V:** Increases the chance to find Seasonings by **+1–5%** [12].
*   **Fortunate Feasting I-V:** Grants **+5–25 Farming Fortune** during a Harvest Feast [12].
*   **Feast Crashers I-III:** Provides **+2–6 Bonus Pest Chance** during the event [12].
*   **Leftovers I-II:** Grants extra **Celestial Mason Jars** upon reaching the final Donation Tier [12].

### **Recommended Unlock Order**
According to community guides and strategic feedback, players should prioritize unlocks in this general order:

1.  **Abiphone Contacts (Ted and Scott):** Community feedback indicates that "everyone should get this first" because they provide essential information on which crops to farm for maximum Seasonings and allow access to the shop year-round [13, 14].
2.  **Farming Tool (Main Crop):** Acquire and upgrade the tier-3 version of the tool for the crop you farm most often to ensure you can consistently earn Gold medals [6, 8].
3.  **Farming Level Cap:** Focus on this as you approach level 50 to ensure you continue gaining SkyBlock XP and permanent stat boosts [15, 16].
4.  **Freshly Baked Accessory Line:** After securing your contacts, focus on these for the permanent "Rare Crop" chance buffs [10, 14].
5.  **Extra Farming Fortune:** Purchase these tiers incrementally to boost your contest scores and total crop yield [5, 17].
6.  **Carnival Perks (Harvest Feast Specific):** Focus on **Fortunate Feasting** and **Natural Talent** to maximize rewards during the seasonal event windows [12].

## Q33: How do Unique Gold Medals work and why do they matter for raising the Farming level cap beyond 50?

**Answer:**

Based on the sources provided, here is how gold medals work and their relationship to the Farming level cap:

### **How Gold Medals Work**
Gold medals are a reward earned through **Jacob's Farming Contests**, which are held every 40 minutes for three random crops [1].
*   **Ranking:** To earn a gold medal, a player must finish in the **top 10%** of all participants for that specific crop during the 20-minute contest window [1].
*   **Rewards:** Along with the medal, players receive **25 Jacob's Tickets** and a **Turbo-Crop enchantment book** (e.g., Turbo-Wheat) corresponding to the crop farmed [1, 2].
*   **Exchange:** These medals and tickets can be used at **Anita**, an NPC located in the Farm Merchant's Dwelling who handles medal-based exchanges [3, 4].

### **Raising the Farming Level Cap Beyond 50**
Originally, Farming (like many skills) was capped at level 50 [5]. The introduction of **Jacob’s Farming Contests** was the primary update that expanded the skill to **Farming 60** [4].

While the provided sources do not explicitly use the phrase "Unique Gold Medals" to describe the level-up process, they establish the necessary components:
*   **Skill Extension:** The "Farming 60" milestone is directly linked to the implementation of Jacob's Contests and the rewards they provide [4].
*   **Progression Logic:** Reaching level caps beyond 50 generally requires specialized items or achievements. For example, in a parallel system for the Fishing skill, players must collect **Unique Golden Pearls** from different islands to raise their Fishing level cap beyond 50 [6, 7]. 
*   **Role of Anita:** Anita is the designated NPC for these skill-capping rewards and exchanges [3, 4]. 

**Note for Players:** In the broader game meta (information not fully detailed in these specific text excerpts), a "Unique" gold medal refers to the first time you earn a gold medal in a specific crop. Each unique medal allows you to purchase an increase for your Farming level cap from Anita, up to the current maximum of 60.

### **Tips for High Contest Ranking**
To secure a gold medal and progress your level cap, the sources recommend:
*   **Specialized Tools:** Use designated tools for each crop (like the Fungi Cutter for mushrooms or specialized Hoes) rather than general gardening hoes, as they provide significantly more Farming Fortune [8, 9].
*   **Greenhouse Mutations:** Harvesting mutations in the Greenhouse can provide a massive head start in a contest. For example, breaking a **Dusk Bloom** or **Chocoberry** mutation during a contest can yield millions of crop slices instantly, often guaranteeing a Diamond or Gold ranking [10].
*   **Farming Fortune Buffs:** Temporary buffs from NPCs like **Maeve** (Harvest Harbinger 5 potion) or emptying your vacuum bag at **Pest Hunter Phillip** can provide hundreds of additional Farming Fortune to boost your contest output [11-13].

## Q34: Explain the Garden system from the beginning. How do you unlock it, how does XP work, what are the 15 levels, and what do you unlock at each level?

**Answer:**

The Garden system is a specialized farming dimension in Hypixel SkyBlock that allows you to build custom, infinite farms for various crops [1, 2].

### **How to Unlock the Garden**
To begin your journey in the Garden, you must first reach **SkyBlock Level 5** [1]. Once you hit this level, an NPC named **Sam** will appear on your private island [1]. Speaking with her will teleport you to the Garden, where you will complete a series of introductory quests to learn the basic loop of clearing plots and planting crops [1, 3].

### **How Garden XP Works**
Garden XP is the currency used to level up your Garden, and it is primarily earned through two methods:
*   **Crop Milestones:** As you break crops within the Garden, you progress through milestones for that specific crop [4]. Each milestone reached rewards you with **Garden Experience**, Farming Experience, and SkyBlock XP [4]. Note that milestones only progress while farming inside the Garden, not on public islands like the Barn [4].
*   **Visitor Offers:** Various NPCs (visitors) will arrive at your Garden and wait by your desk [1, 5]. They will offer rewards—such as **Copper**, Farming Wisdom, and **Garden XP**—in exchange for specific crops they request [1, 5]. Visitors will never ask for a crop you have not yet unlocked [5].

### **Major Garden Level Milestones (Unlocks)**
While the sources do not provide a 1–15 sequential list for every single level, they highlight several critical level-based unlocks:

*   **Level 0 (Start):** You begin with only the **Wheat** crop available [1].
*   **Level 4:** You unlock **Chips**, which provide various buffs to your Garden [6]. An NPC named **Jeff** will appear to introduce the system and give you your first chip [6].
*   **Level 5:** You unlock **Pests** and the **Vacuum** system [3, 7]. At this point, farming crops can spawn pests that reduce your farming fortune if left uncollected [8].
*   **Level 6:** You unlock the **Melon** crop [9].
*   **Level 12:** You unlock the **Greenhouse** [10]. This allows you to build a specialized structure where you can grow **Mutations** (special plant variants like Gloom Gourds) by planting specific crops adjacent to one another [10, 11].
*   **Level 14:** You unlock **Taylor**, a Mythic Visitor who offers unique cosmetics like the Fast Food Barn Skin or the Jelly Greenhouse Skin in exchange for rare farming materials [12, 13].

**Additional Progression Features:**
*   **Composter:** Used to turn organic matter (crops) and fuel into **Compost**, which is required to unlock new plots and upgrade the Greenhouse [3, 14].
*   **Sky Mart:** A shop where you use Copper (earned from visitors) to buy specialized farming tools, the **Lotus equipment** set, and Vacuum upgrades [15-17].
*   **Farming Tools:** Each crop has a designated tool (e.g., Euclid's Wheat Hoe) that levels up from 0 to 40 (or 50 with capsules), increasing your farming fortune as it gains tool XP [15, 18, 19].

## Q35: How does the plot system work in the Garden? How many plots are there total, how do you unlock them, and how much Farming Fortune do they give?

**Answer:**

The plot system in the Garden is a central progression mechanic that allows you to expand your farming area and customize your crop production. Based on the sources, here is how the system works and how you can unlock them:

### **How the Plot System Works**
*   **Customization:** The Garden is divided into various plots where you can choose which specific crop to farm in each area [1]. You can design your own farms or use presets to fill these plots [2, 3].
*   **Clearing Plots:** When you first unlock a plot or a new crop, you must physically go to the plot and "clear" it before it can be used for farming [1]. 
*   **Infrastructure:** You can build across multiple plots to create "infinite" farms (farms that allow crops to regenerate by the time you finish a full circuit) or build vertically to maximize space [2].
*   **The Greenhouse:** Once you reach **Garden Level 12**, you can build a Greenhouse on any of your open plots to grow special mutations [4, 5].

### **How to Unlock Plots**
*   **Garden Access:** You first unlock the Garden and the NPC Sam at **SkyBlock Level 5** [6].
*   **Unlocking Requirement:** Unlocking additional plots requires a significant amount of **Compost** [1, 7].
*   **Producing Compost:** Compost is created in the **Composter** using two main components:
    *   **Organic Matter:** Any type of crop can be used as organic matter [7].
    *   **Fuel:** Items designated as fuel are required to run the composter. **Sunflower Oil** is cited as a commonly used fuel source, though fuel can also be purchased from the Sky Mart [1, 7, 8].
*   **Garden Leveling:** To expand further and unlock higher-tier crops like Melons (Garden Level 6) or Pumpkins, you must earn Garden Experience by completing **Crop Milestones** and **Visitor Offers** [6, 9, 10].

### **Total Plots and Farming Fortune**
*   **Total Plots:** The provided sources mention that players can unlock and manage multiple plots (and up to three Greenhouses), but they **do not explicitly state the total number of plots** available in the entire Garden [1, 11]. (Note: Outside of the sources, the Garden typically consists of a 5x5 grid totaling 25 plots).
*   **Farming Fortune Rewards:** The sources note that "more plots equals more crops being able to be farmed," but they **do not specify the exact amount of Farming Fortune granted per plot unlock** [1]. Farming Fortune is instead primarily discussed as being earned through armor sets like **Fermento** or **Helianthis**, specialized tools, and **Garden Chips** [12-15].

If you would like more specific details on the various farming armor sets or the composter upgrade paths mentioned in the sources, feel free to ask!

## Q36: Explain Garden crop upgrades. How many tiers are there, how much FF does each tier give, what does it cost (copper), and which crop upgrades are highest priority?

**Answer:**

Garden crop upgrades are permanent bonuses for specific crops that can be purchased at the **Garden Desk** [1]. While the provided sources focus heavily on the **2026 Greenhouse and Harvest Feast updates**, they offer the following information regarding Garden progression and crop-specific upgrades:

### **Garden Crop Upgrades Overview**
*   **Location:** Upgrades are purchased via the Garden Desk [1].
*   **Mechanism:** They are often tied to **Crop Milestones**, which track the total amount of a specific crop you have broken while in the Garden [2]. 
*   **Rewards:** Reaching these milestones and purchasing upgrades provides **Garden Experience**, **Farming Experience**, and **SkyBlock XP** [1, 2].
*   **Tiers and Cost:** The sources do not provide a full table for the standard nine tiers of crop upgrades, but they note that the first several levels are **"really cheap"** in terms of copper and should be acquired early for the SkyBlock XP [1, 3].

### **Highest Priority Upgrades**
According to the sources, your priority for upgrading crops should be based on your current farming goals:

1.  **Early Game XP Padding:** You should initially upgrade **all crops equally** for the first few levels because they provide easy SkyBlock XP at a very low cost [1, 3].
2.  **Pest Hunting Meta:** If you are focusing on the highly profitable pest farming/hunting meta, the **highest priority** crop upgrades are **Sunflower, Mushroom, and Potato** [1]. This is because these crops attract specific pests (like Slugs and Locusts) that drop valuable items [1, 4].
3.  **Active Farming Focus:** Beyond the initial cheap levels, you should prioritize buying upgrades for the **crops you are currently farming the most** to maximize efficiency [1].

### **New 2026 Event-Based Upgrades**
The latest updates have introduced additional "tiers" of rewards related to crop production during the **Harvest Feast** event:

*   **Donation Tiers:** There are **5 tiers** of cumulative seasoning donations (at 5, 25, 75, 150, and 250) [5]. Each tier rewards a **Celestial Mason Jar**, which is a powerful mixin providing **+15 Farming Fortune** [5-7].
*   **Freshly Baked Accessory Line:** This is a new **5-tier** progression line (Talisman, Ring, Artifact, Relic, and Heirloom) [8]. 
    *   **Cost:** Purchased using **Kernels** (a currency earned during the Grand Feast) [8, 9].
    *   **Stats:** These accessories grant a **+1-5% Rare Crop chance** [8].
*   **Feast I Books:** These can be applied to farming tools for **25 Kernels**, providing a **+0.5-2.5% Rare Crop chance** [8].
*   **Farming Tool Tiers:** Farming tools themselves have been revamped and categorized, now having **multiple tiers** (such as Euclid's Wheat Hoe T1/T2/T3) that you craft using Jacob's Tickets and the respective crop [8, 10]. Upgrading these tools to higher tiers increases their rarity and base **Farming Fortune** [11].

## Q37: How does the Garden visitor system work? What visitors should players prioritize and what items do they need?

**Answer:**

The Garden visitor system involves NPCs appearing on your Garden to offer rewards in exchange for specific crops you have unlocked [1, 2]. Completing these offers is a primary way to earn **Garden Experience, Farming Experience, SkyBlock XP, and Copper**, which is the Garden's unique currency used at the Sky Mart [1, 3, 4].

### How the System Works
*   **Arrival and Offers:** Visitors arrive periodically. They will only request crops that the player has already unlocked, meaning early-game players will mostly be asked for wheat [1, 2]. 
*   **Expansion through Progression:** As you increase your Garden level, you unlock new crops and a wider variety of potential visitors, including rarer tiers like Legendary and Mythic [5, 6].
*   **Enhancing Visitor Quality:** You can increase the frequency and rarity of visitors using several methods:
    *   **Copper Accessories:** A line of accessories that directly increases the chance for rare and above visitors [7-9].
    *   **Invisibug Attribute:** This attribute also improves the odds of attracting rare visitors [10].
    *   **Quick Draw Chip:** Found in the Garden's chip system (unlocked at Garden level 4), this makes visitors arrive faster [10, 11].
*   **New Interaction Mechanics:** Recent updates have introduced ways to manipulate the visitor pool:
    *   **Charming:** Gifting a **Carrot-Colored Vinyl Set** (filled with five pest vinyls) to a visitor "charms" them, causing them to provide better rewards, more copper, and "gratitude" materials for accessories on their next visit [12-14].
    *   **Scaring Off:** The **Brown Mushroom Paste** can be given to a visitor to scare them away for 24 hours without providing a reward, which is useful for filtering out NPCs you don't want to see [15-17].
    *   **Copper Tipping:** Giving a **Gold Tip Jar** to the NPC Sam permanently unlocks a tip jar with a 100-copper capacity that visitors can contribute to [15, 17].

### Visitors to Prioritize
Players should focus on high-rarity visitors that offer unique progression items or valuable materials:
*   **Rhino (Mythic):** Highly prioritized because he can reward **Dedication 4** books, which are essential for high-end farming fortune [18, 19].
*   **Maeve (Mythic):** Provides **Harvest Harbinger potions**, which grant temporary but significant farming fortune buffs [18, 20].
*   **Taylor (Mythic):** Unlocked at Garden Level 14, she offers exclusive cosmetics like the **Fast Food Barn Skin** or the **Jelly Greenhouse Skin** [6, 12, 21].
*   **Carrot King Avatar:** This special visitor asks for carrots and a clover in exchange for an **upgraded clover** [22, 23].
*   **Legendary Visitors:** Generally worth prioritizing to level up your visitor milestones, which in turn buffs your **Lotus Equipment** stats [24, 25].

### Items Needed for Visitors
To successfully complete offers and optimize the system, you will need:
*   **Specific Crops:** Always maintain a stock of all unlocked crops to fulfill standard requests [1].
*   **Interaction Items:**
    *   **Carrot-Colored Vinyl Set:** Requires 9 feast carrot drops and 15 enchanted oak wood to craft; it must be filled with pest vinyls before gifting [12].
    *   **Brown Mushroom Paste:** Crafted with 3 enchanted brown mushroom blocks and 12 feast mushroom drops [15].
    *   **Gold Tip Jar:** Crafted with 96 enchanted red mushroom blocks and 128 feast mushroom drops [15].
*   **Specific Materials for Taylor:** Depending on the cosmetic skin she offers, she may require:
    *   **Barn Skin:** Deepfries, Salted Sunflower Seeds, and Enchanted Baked Potatoes [6, 21].
    *   **Greenhouse Skin:** Floral Gelatin, Melon Juice, and Compacted Wild Roses [6, 21].
*   **Carrot King Requirements:** You will need carrots and a **Lucky Clover** for his upgrade quest [22].

## Q38: What is copper in the Garden and how do players earn it efficiently? What is the best way to spend copper?

**Answer:**

**Copper** is the primary currency used in the **Garden** for progression and upgrades [1].

### **How to Earn Copper Efficiently**
The most common way to earn copper is by completing **visitor offers**, which involve giving visitors the specific crops they request [1]. To maximize your earnings, you should utilize the following methods:

*   **Analyze Mutations:** The first time you analyze a new type of mutation in **Jake's lab**, you receive a "ton of copper" [2].
*   **Ladybug Attribute Shard:** Equipping this attribute can grant you **10% more copper** from visitors [3].
*   **Vinyl Sets:** Giving a visitor a **Carrot-Colored Vinyl Set** (filled with pest vinyls) causes them to reward you with more copper the next time they return [4, 5].
*   **Tip Jars:** You can craft a **Fungal Tip Jar** to increase your copper capacity, or participate in the **Harvest Feast** event to unlock a permanent **gold tip jar** through Sam, which allows visitors to tip you copper [6-8].
*   **Copper Accessories:** A new line of copper accessories increases the chance of encountering **rare or higher-tier visitors**, which likely leads to better copper rewards over time [9, 10].

### **The Best Ways to Spend Copper**
Copper is essential for upgrading your farming efficiency and gear. The most effective uses for your copper include:

*   **Gardening Hoes:** Early on, you should spend 25 copper to get the **Basic** and **Advanced Gardening Hoes**, which provide a baseline of farming fortune [11].
*   **Crop-Specific Tools:** For 250 copper each, you can purchase designated tools for specific crops at the **Sky Mart** to significantly increase your yield for that crop [11].
*   **Composter Upgrades:** Copper is required to upgrade the **Composter**, which is vital for producing compost needed to unlock new plots and build the **Greenhouse** [12, 13].
*   **Vacuum Upgrades:** Upgrading your vacuum is critical for **pest farming**, with the final upgrade (the Infinitive Vacuum Hooverus) costing **2,500 copper** [14, 15].
*   **Copper Accessory Line:** This new accessory line is a priority for end-game players looking to maximize their **Magical Power** and visitor rarity [9, 10].

## Q39: What is the Pesterminator enchantment and how does it interact with Garden pests? Is it worth using?

**Answer:**

Based on the sources, the **Pesterminator** appears to refer to the **Sprayinator** tool (often nicknamed or transcribed as "pterminator" in guides), while its associated enchantment is likely the **Bug Blender**.

### **What is the Pesterminator (Sprayinator) and Bug Blender?**
*   **Sprayinator (Tool):** This item is essential for targeted pest farming. It allows you to "spray" your plots with specific baits to attract certain pests [1-3]. For example, setting it to **Plant Matter** increases the spawn chance of **Slugs** and **Locusts** [2, 4, 5].
*   **Bug Blender (Enchantment):** This is a new enchantment specifically for the **Vacuum** [6]. 
    *   **Effect:** It grants **+20 Farming Fortune** when vacuuming pests per level, capping at **+100 Farming Fortune** at level 5 [6, 7].
    *   **Obtaining:** It is crafted using **3 Enchanted Paper** and **8 Feast Cocoa drops** [7].

### **How does it interact with Garden pests?**
The Pesterminator (Sprayinator) and the Bug Blender enchantment are part of a specialized "Pest Hunting" meta designed to maximize rare drops:
1.  **Attraction:** The Sprayinator ensures the pests you want to farm (like Slugs for money or Locusts for specific drops) spawn more frequently [2, 8].
2.  **Increased Loot:** When you vacuum the pests, the **Bug Blender** enchantment multiplies the items dropped by providing a massive boost to **Farming Fortune on pests** [6].
3.  **Efficiency:** It is often paired with items like the **Disco Destination**, which helps manage how pests load into the Garden, and the **Hedgehog pet**, which grants additional fortune and allows you to one-shot certain pests like **Earthworms** [9-11].

### **Is it worth using?**
**Yes, it is highly worth using** if you are focused on profit. In early 2026, **Pest Farming** has become one of the most consistent and accessible high-tier money-making methods in the game [12, 13].
*   **High Yield:** A maxed setup utilizing these items can generate **60 million to 90 million coins per hour**, especially during **Mayor Finnegan's** events [13, 14].
*   **Active Profit:** Unlike minions, which have been outclassed by active methods, the Bug Blender/Sprayinator setup allows you to "turn time into coins" very efficiently [15, 16].
*   **Rare Drops:** Since rare drops from pests are highly valued for new 2026 recipes (such as the **Pesthunter set** or **Lotus upgrades**), the extra fortune from the enchantment is considered "goated" by the community [17-19].

In summary, while the setup can be expensive and requires high farming levels, the **Bug Blender enchantment** and **Sprayinator** are vital for anyone serious about maximizing their Garden profit [13, 14].

## Q40: Explain the Farming level cap system. How do players raise the cap from 50 to 60, and what Jacob milestones are required?

**Answer:**

In Hypixel SkyBlock, the Farming level cap system allows players to progress their Farming skill beyond the initial limit of level 50, eventually reaching a maximum of **level 60** [1, 2].

### Raising the Farming Level Cap (50 to 60)
The ability to raise the Farming cap was introduced during the **Farm Merchant's Dwelling** update [2]. To increase the cap from 50 toward 60, players must engage with the **Jacob’s Farming Contests** system [2, 3].

*   **Participation in Contests:** Farming contests occur every 40 minutes and last for 20 minutes, focusing on three random crops [3, 4]. Players compete to farm as much of a specific crop as possible within the time limit [3].
*   **Earning Currency:** Based on their performance, players earn **medals** (Bronze, Silver, or Gold) and **Jacob's Tickets** [3, 5].
    *   **Gold Medals:** Top 10% of participants [3].
    *   **Silver Medals:** Top 30% of participants [3].
    *   **Bronze Medals:** Top 60% of participants [3].
*   **Purchasing Upgrades:** Players use these medals and tickets at **Anita** (located in the Farm Merchant's Dwelling) to purchase level cap increases [6, 7]. Each purchase typically increases the Farming level cap by one, up to level 60.

### Requirements and Milestones
While the sources do not provide a granular list of every medal cost per level, they establish that the progression is strictly tied to **Jacob's Farming Contests** and the acquisition of medals [2, 3].

*   **Medal Milestones:** To reach level 60, a player must earn a significant number of **Gold Medals**. Higher level cap increases generally require Gold Medals as a primary currency.
*   **Greenhouse Strategy:** To more easily reach the high brackets (like Gold or the newer Diamond bracket) required for these medals, players often utilize the **Greenhouse**. Breaking fully-grown mutations (like **Dusk Bloom** or **Chocoberries**) at the start of a contest provides an "insane head start" in the crop count, making it much easier to secure a top placement [8, 9].
*   **Farming Tools:** To be competitive in these contests, players must invest in specialized farming tools (such as the **Euclid's Wheat Hoe** or **Newton Nether Warts Hoe**) that can be upgraded through three tiers using Jacob's Tickets and the respective crops [7, 10].

It is important to distinguish these requirements from **Crop Milestones**. Crop Milestones are individual progress trackers for every crop type that grant Farming and SkyBlock XP based on the total amount of that specific crop harvested; they do not directly raise the overall level cap but are essential for leveling up within the cap [11, 12].

---

## Q41: Which crops give the most coins per hour at early game (no Garden, Farming 1-20)?

**Answer:**

For players in the early game (Farming levels 1–20) who have not yet unlocked the Garden, the most consistent way to generate coins is by farming **Wheat** in the Hub [1, 2]. 

### **Primary Crop: Wheat**
**Wheat** is categorized as the most beginner-friendly and reliable money-making method for new profiles [2, 3]. 
*   **Ease of Access:** You can begin harvesting wheat in the **Hub Wheat Fields** immediately upon joining the game [4, 5]. 
*   **NPC vs. Bazaar Profit:** Initially, you can sell wheat and seeds to NPCs for "easy starter cash" to afford early gear [1, 2]. However, once you reach **SkyBlock Level 7** and unlock the **Bazaar**, your profits will increase significantly [6, 7]. For example, while an NPC might buy wheat for 3 coins, players on the Bazaar may pay **10+ coins per piece**, making it the backbone of a new player's economy [8].
*   **Collection Benefits:** Farming wheat allows you to reach **Wheat Collection III**, which is necessary to craft the **Farm Suit**, providing early-game survivability [4, 9].

### **Alternative Early Options**
While wheat is the standard for raw coin generation, other public island crops can be used to progress your account:
*   **Carrots and Potatoes:** These can be found at **The Barn** [10]. While primarily used to unlock early collections and minions for SkyBlock XP, they provide an alternative to wheat if the Hub fields are over-contested [10].
*   **Mushrooms:** Collecting **mushroom blocks** in the **Mushroom Desert** is recommended as a slightly more "active" money-making option [11]. To maximize profit here, you should add the **Silk Touch** enchantment to your tool to harvest the blocks directly [11].
*   **Sand:** Although technically a mining resource, digging sand in the Mushroom Desert is cited as a fast way to level skills and obtain materials that are an "easy early sell" on the Bazaar [12, 13].

### **Maximizing Early Profits**
To improve your coins per hour at this stage, you should focus on the following:
*   **Tools:** Purchase a **Rookie Hoe** from the Farm Merchant and enchant it with **Harvesting** to increase your crop yields [13].
*   **SkyBlock Level 7:** Prioritize reaching Level 7 as quickly as possible to transition from selling to NPCs to selling on the **Bazaar**, which offers much higher market rates for your crops [8, 14].
*   **Booster Cookie:** If you can obtain a **Booster Cookie**, it allows you to access the Bazaar and Auction House remotely via commands, significantly reducing the downtime spent traveling between the fields and the market [15].

---

## Q42: Which crops give the most coins per hour at mid game (Garden level 3-7, Farming 20-40)?

**Answer:**

In the mid-game (defined as **Garden levels 3–7** and **Farming levels 20–40**), the most profitable farming methods shift from raw crop selling to a focus on **rare drops**, **Pest Farming**, and **Greenhouse mutations**.

The following crops and methods yield the highest coins per hour for players in this range:

### 1. Pest Farming (The Current Meta)
As of early 2026, **Pest Farming** is widely considered the most efficient money-making method in the Garden [1, 2].
*   **Yield:** A mid-game setup (using **Squash or Fermento armor**) can generate approximately **40M–50M coins per hour** [3, 4].
*   **Strategy:** Players should farm crops to trigger pest spawns, then use a vacuum (like the **Infinitive Vacuum Hooverous**) and a **Hedgehog pet** to kill them for high-value drops like **Shards** and **Vinyls** [5-7].
*   **Optimizing:** Using a **Mosquito pet** can increase bonus pest chance significantly, and performing "rod swaps" or "equipment swaps" when pests spawn can further boost profits [8-10].

### 2. Greenhouse Mutations
The Greenhouse, unlocked at **Garden level 12** but accessible for progression planning earlier, offers high-yield passive income [5].
*   **Yield:** **40M–50M coins per hour** through methods like **Ashreath** or **Witherloom** mutation layouts [1].
*   **Profit Source:** Most profit comes from harvesting mutations every few hours, which yields an "obscene" amount of crops when multiplied by high Farming Fortune [11, 12]. **Ashreath** is noted as being about 20% better for raw NPC sell value compared to other common mutations [13].

### 3. Rare Drop Progression (Armor-Based)
Your profit is heavily dictated by the rare items dropped while wearing specific armor sets [14]:
*   **Melon Armor (Farming 25+):** Focus on **Wheat, Carrots, or Potatoes** to drop **Croppies**.
*   **Crappie Armor (Farming 30+):** Focus on **Cocoa Beans, Melons, or Pumpkins** to drop **Squash**.
*   **Squash Armor (Farming 35+):** Focus on **Sugar Cane, Nether Wart, Mushrooms, or Cactus** to drop **Fermentos**.
*   **Bazaar vs. NPC:** In 2026, selling these rare materials on the **Bazaar** is significantly more profitable than selling to NPCs, as player-driven prices for these upgrade materials remain high [15].

### 4. Specialized Crop Recommendations
*   **Mushrooms:** Recommended for players prioritized on **leveling** to Farming 40+ quickly, as they provide the most EXP per crop broken [16].
*   **Sunflowers & Moonflowers:** For standard (non-Ironman) profiles, these are often the best crops to farm for direct Bazaar profit due to high demand for materials used in the **Harvest Feast** event and new gear upgrades [17].

### Summary of Coins per Hour (Mid-Game Estimates)
| Method | Estimated Coins/hr | Requirements |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Pest Farming** | 40M - 50M | Fermento/Squash setup [1, 4] |
| **Greenhouse Mutations**| 40M - 50M | Ashreath/Witherloom layouts [1] |
| **Standard Crop Farming**| 15M - 25M | Squash Armor + Tier 2 Hoes [18, 19] |
| **Pelt Farming** | 50M - 70M | Finnegan active + Talbot gear [20] |

**Important Note:** To maximize these rates, you should aim for **100% Crit Chance** via accessories and Maxwell’s powers to ensure consistency in any combat-related garden activities (like killing pests) [21, 22].

---

## Q43: Which crops give the most coins per hour at endgame (max Garden, Farming 50-60, Helianthus armor)?

**Answer:**

At the absolute endgame in Hypixel SkyBlock (max Garden, Farming 50–60, and Helianthus armor), raw coin generation is generally maximized through **Pest Farming** and the **Greenhouse**, rather than just farming a single crop for NPC sell value [1-3].

### 1. Pest Farming (The Highest Yield Method)
Pest farming is currently the most profitable active farming method for endgame players. While farming any crop, players intentionally spawn and hunt pests to earn coins from rare drops, attributes, and vendor materials [3, 4].

*   **Coins per Hour:** 
    *   **40M – 50M/hr** under normal conditions [5, 6].
    *   **60M – 90M/hr** when **Mayor Finnegan** or **Aura** is active with perks like "Pest Eradicator" or "Grand Feast" [1, 2, 6].
*   **Optimal Crops to Farm for Pests:**
    *   **Wheat:** Preferred by many endgame players (like those on Ironman) because it yields **XP Tool Capsules** (worth 100k each) and targets **Slug pests** for high-value drops [4, 7].
    *   **Sunflowers/Mushrooms:** These are often used to target specific pests like **Locusts** or **Slugs**, which drop high-value items for the Bazaar or NPC selling [8, 9].
*   **Endgame Setup:** Requires Helianthus armor (Manted reforge), a hypermaxed vacuum (**Infinity Vacuum Hooverous** with the BD reforge), and a **Rose Dragon** or **Hedgehog** pet [10-13].

### 2. Greenhouse Mutations (Best Semi-Passive Profit)
For players looking for "set and forget" profit, the Greenhouse provides significant income through crop mutations.

*   **Top Crop:** **Ashreath** is cited as the best mutation for raw coins, yielding approximately **20% more profit** per harvest than other common mutations when sold to NPCs [14].
*   **Coins per Hour:** Semi-passive yields can reach **40M – 50M per hour** of active management (clearing green houses every few hours) [1].
*   **Optimization:** Maximizing **Growth Speed** (to a 1 hour 48 minute timer) and **Yield Bonus** by placing all 12 unique crops in the greenhouse is essential for these rates [15, 16].

### 3. Harvest Feast "In-Season" Crops
During the **Harvest Feast** event (Early to Late Autumn), three specific crops enter "harvesting season" every SkyBlock month [17, 18].

*   **Profit Factor:** Farming these crops allows for a 1/18,000 chance to drop unique materials like **Deepfries** (Potatoes) or **Cane Knots** (Sugar Cane) [19, 20].
*   **Meta Impact:** These materials are used to craft high-demand endgame items, such as the **Hashbrown reforge** or **Thermodynamic armor**, often making the "In-Season" crops the most profitable to farm actively for Bazaar profit during the event [19, 21].

### 4. Summary of Best Crops
| Category | Best Crop/Mutation | Estimated Yield |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Active MMM** | **Wheat** (targeting Pests) | 60M – 90M/hr (w/ Finnegan) [1, 2, 4] |
| **Bazaar Profit** | **Sunflower, Moonflower, Wild Rose** | Varies (High Bazaar demand) [22] |
| **NPC Profit** | **Ashreath** (Greenhouse Mutation) | 40M – 50M/hr (equivalent) [1, 14] |
| **Event Profit** | **Current "In-Season" Crop** | Variable spike based on rare drops [19, 20] |

**Important Note:** To reach these rates, players must utilize **equipment swapping** (switching to higher-fortune sets like the **Blossom set** when killing pests) and maintain high **Magical Power** with tuning points set to **Strength** to buff the **Mushroom Cow** pet if used [12, 23, 24].

## Q44: Deep dive on Wheat farming: what is the coins/hr at different FF levels, what enchants/pets are best, and what collection milestones matter?

**Answer:**

Wheat farming in 2026 has evolved from a simple beginner task into a high-profit activity integrated with **Pest Farming** and the new **Harvest Feast** event.

### **Coins per Hour and Farming Fortune (FF)**
Profit in wheat farming is now heavily tied to **Pest Farming** and seasonal event drops rather than just raw crop sales.

*   **Early Game:** Farming wheat in the Hub fields is a reliable method for starting cash and unlocking basic gear like the Farm Suit [1, 2].
*   **Mid-to-Late Game (Pest-Focused):** With a maxed setup, players can generate **40-50 million coins per hour** through pest farming [3, 4].
*   **Peak Efficiency (Finnegan):** During Mayor Finnegan’s term, rates can climb to **80-90 million coins per hour** due to reduced pest cooldowns and specific event perks [5-7].
*   **FF Requirements:** High-tier players often aim for over **2,000 Farming Fortune** to reach these rates [7, 8]. To make roughly **40M/hr** with wheat, players utilize a combination of high FF and "Manted" armor sets to maximize rare drops [9, 10].

### **Best Enchantments and Pets**
The meta for tools has shifted with the "Farming Tool" recategorization, where hoes and axes now share the same enchantments and reforges [11].

**Best Enchantments:**
*   **Harvesting VI:** The standard primary enchantment for hoes [12, 13].
*   **Turbo-Wheat V:** Provides up to **+25 Wheat Fortune** [14].
*   **Crop Fever (Ultimate):** A proc-based enchantment that grants **+100 Farming Fortune** and increases the chance for rare drops like Crappies or Fermentos by 15% [15, 16].
*   **Dedication IV:** Essential for scaling fortune based on crop milestones [16, 17].
*   **Feast I-V:** A new enchantment from the Harvest Feast event that increases the chance of finding rare crops [18].

**Best Pets:**
*   **Rabbit:** The definitive early-game pet for gaining **Farming Wisdom** and leveling up quickly [19].
*   **Elephant:** A top-tier choice for raw Farming Fortune; a **Mythic rarity** version is now obtainable using "Littlefoot Fluff" [20, 21].
*   **Mushroom Cow:** The second-best pet for FF, particularly when paired with a high-strength build using **Acacia Birdhouse** power stones [22].
*   **Hedgehog:** Mandatory for **Pest Farming**, as it provides extra fortune on pest kills and allows you to one-shot earthworms [8, 23].
*   **Rose Dragon:** The absolute **Best-in-Slot (BiS)** pet for Farming Fortune, though it costs over 2 billion coins [4, 23].
*   **Slug/Mosquito:** Used primarily during the spawning phase of pest farming to increase **Bonus Pest Chance** [24].

### **Important Collection & Progression Milestones**
Wheat collection remains the foundation of a player's agricultural progression in the Garden.

*   **Wheat III:** Unlocks the **Farm Suit**, the first armor set with Farming Fortune [25, 26].
*   **Agronomy Sacks:** Unlocked through wheat collection to help manage inventory while farming [14].
*   **Crop Milestones:** Breaking wheat contributes to milestones that reward **Garden XP, Farming XP, and SkyBlock XP**, capping at 50,000 crops for the final milestone tier [27].
*   **Lotus Equipment:** Unlocked as you progress through the Garden, these items (Bracelet, Belt, Necklace, Cloak) scale their FF based on the **total number of visitor offers** you have completed [28].
*   **Harvest Feast (New):** If wheat is "In-Season" during Autumn, it has a 1/2,250 chance to drop **Seasoning** for the communal stew and a 1/18,000 chance to drop a **Cornucopia** [29-31].
    *   **Cornucopia Crystal:** Crafted from wheat drops, it makes farming minions work 10% faster [30].
    *   **Not Very Moldy Bread:** A consumable providing **+30 Farming Wisdom** for 60 minutes [32].

---

## Q45: Deep dive on Sugar Cane farming: setup, coins/hr at different stages, and tips for maximizing yield.

**Answer:**

Sugar Cane farming has evolved into a multi-layered activity in 2026, transitioning from a simple "semi-AFK" money-maker to a highly optimized method that integrates the **Greenhouse**, **Pest Farming**, and the **Harvest Feast** event.

### **Sugar Cane Farming Setup**

To maximize yield, your setup should progress through these stages:

*   **Tools:** The **Turing Sugar Cane Hoe (T1, T2, T3)** is the primary tool [1]. At a minimum, it should have **Harvesting V/VI**, **Cultivating**, and **Turbo-Cane V** (or VI/VII if obtained via the Harvest Feast) [2-4]. Hypermaxed tools should reach **Level 50** using tool XP capsules and use **Perfect Paradox Gemstones** [2, 5].
*   **Armor Progression:**
    *   **Early:** Farm Suit or Farm Armor [6].
    *   **Mid-Game:** **Squash Armor** is the critical mid-game set because it allows **Fermento** to drop while farming Sugar Cane [7].
    *   **End-Game:** **Fermento Armor** (to drop Helianthus) or the new **Helianthus Armor**, which is currently the best farming set in the game [8, 9].
*   **Equipment:** The **Lotus Set** (bracelet, belt, necklace, cloak) is standard, providing fortune that scales with total visitor offers [10]. This is eventually upgraded to the **Blossom Set** for higher base fortune [8, 11].
*   **Pets:**
    *   **Rabbit:** Best for early-game leveling to reach higher farming wisdom [12].
    *   **Elephant:** The standard for raw farming fortune [13].
    *   **Mushroom Cow:** Often preferred for pure profit if you have high **Strength**, as it grants additional fortune and mushroom drops [14].
*   **Reforges:** **Blessed** is recommended for more wisdom/fortune early, while **Bountiful** is used for raw coin generation [4, 15].

---

### **Coins/hr at Different Stages**

Profitability is heavily dictated by your level of activity (standard farming vs. pest hunting):

*   **Mid-Game (Standard):** Around **10M–20M coins/hr**. At this stage, you are primarily earning through raw Sugar Cane sales and **Fermento drops** from Squash armor [7, 16].
*   **Late-Game (Max Fortune):** Approximately **40M–50M coins/hr**. This is achieved with maxed Helianthus armor, a Level 100 Mushroom Cow pet, and high-tier equipment [17, 18].
*   **Active Pest Farming (Finnegan Active):** Can reach **60M–90M coins/hr**. By incorporating an active pest-hunting strategy (using a vacuum and rod-swapping to a **Hedgehog** pet), Sugar Cane becomes a vehicle for spawning high-value pests [19, 20].
*   **Harvest Feast Event:** During the three months of **Autumn**, Sugar Cane can drop **Cane Knots** (1/18,000 chance) [21, 22]. These are used to craft the **Speed Relic**, **Unalloyed Speed**, and **Thermodynamic Armor**, creating significant profit spikes [21, 23, 24].

---

### **Tips for Maximizing Yield**

1.  **Use a Sundial:** Sugar Cane farming requires precise movement. The **Sundial** allows you to set a custom speed at the garden desk, ensuring you maintain the optimal **20 blocks per second** without overshooting the crops [9, 13].
2.  **The "Glass Ceiling" Strategy:** Build a glass roof over your Sugar Cane farm. This forces **pests** to spawn on top of the glass where they are easier to see and vacuum up, reducing downtime spent searching for them [25, 26].
3.  **Empty Your Vacuum Bag:** Talking to **Pest Hunter Phillip** to empty your vacuum bag provides a temporary **+200 Farming Fortune** buff (up to **+400** if the **Hypercharge chip** is maxed) [27, 28].
4.  **Harvest During Autumn:** Focus your Sugar Cane efforts during the **Harvest Feast** (Early, Mid, and Late Autumn) to drop **Seasonings** for the communal stew and **Cane Knots** for high-value crafting [29, 30].
5.  **Garden Chips:** Prioritize upgrading the **Vermin Vaporizer** chip (for more pests) and the **Crop Shot** chip (for flat fortune) using **Sodust** gathered while farming [27, 31, 32].
6.  **Pet Rules for Efficiency:** Set an **Auto Pet** rule to equip your **Elephant/Cow** when you gain farming XP, and a **Hedgehog** when you use a vacuum to kill pests. This ensures you always have the best stats for the current action without manual swapping [33-35].
